- **gmem-ask**: Explains gmem — why it exists, every kind of memory, the memory lifecycle, which skill to use when, and the four install modes. - **gmem-bye**: End-of-session checkpoint that checks whether anything from the session belongs in a note, memory, retro, or learning, and records only what qualifies. - **gmem-check**: Checks gmem memory without changing it — whether files follow gmem's rules (layout, file names, complete indexes, one note per topic) and whether their format is right (required frontmatter, links that resolve), using typdoc when it is set up. Reports each finding with the skill that fixes it. Use before committing memory, after hand edits, at the start of gmem-tidy, or when the user asks to "check memory", "validate memory", "lint memory", or "is my memory OK". Read-only — to fix what it finds, use gmem-tidy, gmem-judge or gmem-migrate. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem/skills/gmem-check) · ★13)
- **gmem-development**: Guides development in the Graphmem/gmem Rust repository — its layered hexagonal architecture, the check/test/lint/fmt feedback loop, and testing conventions. - **gmem-judge**: Reviews the captured learnings and acts on each one — fixes small problems in place, or raises a proposal when a learning should be promoted, conflicts with a rule or precedent, or is unclear. Use when asked to "judge", "review the learnings", "triage learnings", "vet the inbox", or "clean up captured facts", or after many gmem-learn captures have piled up. Not for writing rules or precedents (a human acts on proposals), not for retros, and not for a full memory audit — use gmem-tidy. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem/skills/gmem-judge) · ★13)
- **gmem-learn**: Saves one durable, timeless fact that was learned from the work itself — a gotcha hit, a constraint discovered, a mistake that turned out to have a general cause — as a learning card in gmem memory. Use whenever something like that turns up mid-work, when pulling lasting facts out of a retro, or when the user says "TIL" or "lesson learned", even if they never say "learning". Not for what happened in a session — use gmem-retro; not for how the project works when the user tells you, or for revising what a topic already says — use gmem-memory. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem/skills/gmem-learn) · ★13)
- **gmem-map**: The map of the whole gmem garden — what kinds of memory exist, which layer wins on conflict, when to recall and validate, which skill records what, and the everyday rhythm. Open it at the start of a session or before non-trivial work in a project that uses gmem, when unsure which gmem skill fits, or when the user asks how memory works here. Read-only; it points to the skills that read and write. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem/skills/gmem-map) · ★13)
- **gmem-memory**: Keeps a curated, editable knowledge base by topic — corrections to past mistakes and reference notes on how things work — revised in place as understanding changes. Use when the user tells you how something works or wants to add to, update, or review what is known about a topic ("remember this", "save this", "add to memory", "update memory", "that's outdated now"). Not for a fact the agent learned from its own work — use gmem-learn; not for current project status — use gmem-note. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem/skills/gmem-memory) · ★13)
- **gmem-migrate**: Migrates older memory into the current gmem layout — from typmem (.typmem/ folders, ~/.typmem/memory/, a single rules.md, old skill names) or the crew layout (locker/memory/, locker/note/, crew-note/crew-memory/crew-good-bye) — with a dry-run plan first and nothing deleted. Manual-only — run as /gmem-migrate. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem-tools/skills/gmem-migrate) · ★13)
- **gmem-note**: Writes or updates a short status note for one project or topic — what is done, open, and next — so a fresh session or another agent can resume without re-explaining. Use when the user asks to save where things stand, "note this down for next time", or "leave a status note". Not for the story of a session — use gmem-retro; not for lasting facts — use gmem-learn. - **gmem-recall**: Looks up what memory already says — rules, precedents, active notes, and open work — that bears on the task at hand. Use before starting non-trivial work, when resuming earlier work, before locking in a consequential decision or plan, or when the user asks "what do we know about X", "have we hit this before", or "check memory first", even if they don't mention memory. Read-only — to write, use gmem-learn, gmem-memory, gmem-retro, gmem-note, or gmem-judge. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem/skills/gmem-recall) · ★13)
- **gmem-retro**: Writes a dated diary entry of a work session — the goal, what happened, decisions, dead ends, and unfinished threads. Use when the user asks to write a retro, log what happened, record today, or capture this session, or right after a meaningful chunk of work concludes, even if they never say "retro". Not for the end-of-session checkpoint — gmem-bye decides whether a retro is needed; not for durable facts — use gmem-learn. ([thaitype/gmem](https://github.com/thaitype/gmem/tree/HEAD/plugins/gmem/skills/gmem-retro) · ★13)
- **gmem-setup**: Sets up or inspects gmem's config, which decides where each kind of memory is stored — memory, notes, rules, precedents, learnings, proposals and retros, each in the project, at user level, or a custom path. Offers four install modes (Central memory, Home Repo Pattern memory, Dedicated agents memory, Custom) and asks where the config file lives. Use when the user wants to set up gmem, pick an install mode, change where any kind is kept, check which config is in effect, check or install the skills gmem-tidy depends on (grilling, wayfinder), or when another gmem skill finds no config.
